Just... what's coming next.\n\nDoctor: When these worries start, are you usually able to control them or stop them from escalating?\n\nPatient: No... not really. Um... like I just said, I can't shut my brain off. Once it starts with that one little thing, I can't stop it from... you know, getting to all the rest of it. It just takes over.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a traumatic event in your life?\n\nPatient: No. Nothing like that. Just... normal life stuff, I guess. Nothing traumatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to calm down or distract yourself from these worries at all, or do they completely take over?\n\nPatient: Not really. And that I'll never get back to a real leadership role. It just spirals, and... no, I can't calm down once it does.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I mean... I start thinking about all the ways it could go wrong, and I just get stuck. \n\nAnd... I don't know, I've kind of stopped going to my soccer games. Haven't been seeing my friends much either. I just... I'm so tense all the time, and I snap at people. It's embarrassing. So it's just easier to stay home.\n\nDoctor: Are there any areas of your life where you feel you don't worry at all?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I can't really think of any... it's pretty much just everywhere, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries are bigger than the problems themselves?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Yeah. It's just... my mind keeps jumping around. \n\nFrom contract work... to the mortgage payments. Or I start wondering if people in the Bethesda tech circles think I'm... damaged goods.
